21: # Citation Discipline — Cross-Cutting Doctrine
22: # Reference: Z:/repos/deps/autoresearch/.claude/skills/autoresearch/SKILL.md (verified 2026-04-18 in parent; deps-shared with sss)
23: # Reference: Z:/claude/.claude/projects/Z--claude/memory/feedback_reference_first_hard_rule.md (parent hard rule, 2026-04-18 — PARENT-ATTRIBUTION; sss memory port pending)
24: # Retracted: ARIS + QWED-AI + lean-ctx anti-pattern refs — none of those repos exist in Z:/repos/deps/
25: 
26: ## Rule: Every new pattern MUST cite its source at file:line depth
27: 
28: 1. **Header comment**: `# Reference: Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/<file>:<line>` or `# Reference: <official-docs-URL>`
29: 2. **Never cite READMEs alone** — cite the actual source code implementation
30: 3. **Star count does NOT indicate quality** — read the source, verify the claim
31: 4. **Verify before trust** — the cited file must still exist AND still say what you cite (labels decay)
32: 5. **Silent fallback = violation** — if expected output doesn't appear (e.g., `embeddings: 0` after `--embeddings`), that's a STOP, not a footnote (parent `feedback_reference_first_hard_rule.md` — PARENT-ATTRIBUTION pending sss memory port)
33: 6. **TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE class for inherited patterns** — when a SOTA pattern is derived from a named author's published work (book / peer-reviewed paper / conference talk), cite verbatim-quote + author-name + publication inline. The named-author cite is TIER-1 primary-source for the PATTERN (not the implementing repo); use alongside repo file:line cites for full provenance. Exemplar: `Z:/repos/deps/mattpocock/skills/README.md` (48,857★, MIT, [VERIFIED 2026-04-30 PM via curl raw GitHub]) quotes Pragmatic Programmer (Thomas & Hunt) for "rate of feedback is your speed limit" (failure-mode #3 framing), Domain-Driven-Design (Eric Evans) for ubiquitous-language pattern (#2 framing), and Extreme Programming Explained (Kent Beck) for "invest in design every day" (#4 framing). Per `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/reference_mattpocock_skills_pattern_extract_2026_04_30.md` for the full mining trace + adoption-candidate ranking. **TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE PROVISIONAL-EXCEPTION** *(round-3 framing per T3 round-2 conf=0.88 ESCALATING finding on commit `d4df352`; supersedes round-1 "full cardinal-rule-1 satisfaction" framing — physical-only sources do NOT fully satisfy committed-tree inspectability)*: physical-only printed-book/journal/conference-publication primary anchor (ISBN + page + verbatim-quote) is a PROVISIONAL-EXCEPTION to cardinal-rule-1 (CLAUDE.md L7), NOT full satisfaction. Cite class downgraded to TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL with REQUIRED metadata: (a) ISBN/DOI + page + verbatim-quote inline; (b) verifier-identity disclosure (who-verified-when, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-04 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`); (c) explicit `non-automatable: true` marker so review hooks know to defer to human sign-off; (d) **HARD-GATE ELIGIBILITY TEST** *(Round-3 amendment a per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "MUST prefer" soft preference framing — preference is not gateable; eligibility is)*: PROVISIONAL admission is admissible ONLY when DOI landing page + accessible PDF / publisher excerpt / archived scan (license-permitting) was SEARCHED AND UNAVAILABLE. The commit body MUST document the search (which DOI/publisher/archive was queried + return shape — e.g., "searched ACM DOI 10.1145/<doi> 2026-05-05 — paywall-only, no excerpt found; searched Internet Archive — no scan; searched publisher excerpt page — none"). Mere preference is INSUFFICIENT — if any auditable surrogate exists (DOI/PDF/excerpt/scan), use it as the normative source instead of the physical-only book cite. e.g., ACM DOI `https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/<doi>` for ACM papers IS auditable when paywall-free. Secondary aggregator URLs (Wikipedia, `https://pragprog.com/tips/`, `https://martinfowler.com/bliki/<topic>.html`, `c2.com/cgi/wiki`) are VERIFICATION PROXIES only — they help re-verify the primary quote without owning the book physically, but are NOT cited as normative source. **Independent named-reviewer sign-off mandate** *(Round-3 amendment b per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "Lower-confidence reviewer-sign-off mandate" sole-orchestrator framing — sole-orchestrator verification is no longer admissible)*: rule edits citing TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL MUST cite a SECOND verifier-identity BEYOND the orchestrator who replayed the verbatim quote against the source — commit body MUST embed BOTH verifier-identity-1 (the orchestrator, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`) AND verifier-identity-2 (the independent named reviewer, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by <human-name> against same book + same page + same quote]`) with explicit `[VERIFIED yyyy-mm-dd by <name> against <source>]` markers for each. The 2-verifier requirement closes the "self-attestation only" gap that round-2 left open. **`non-automatable: true` operator-discipline mandate (manual-only; no hook enforcement YET)** *(Round-4 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS finding on commit `d07ac71`; supersedes Round-3 amendment c "BLOCKING in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape" claim that was prose-only without hook implementation — codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE caught the policy/enforcement drift: rule text claimed automated BLOCKING but no T3/T4/T6 hook, verdict parser, schema, or test changed. Per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md §Outcome A monotone-decline path, downgraded prose closes the drift while the enforcement-hook ship is queued separately)*: when a TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL cite is shipped, the operator MUST: (i) embed `non-automatable: true` marker inline; (ii) embed explicit "no automated enforcement, human gate only" disclosure in commit body acknowledging that automated re-verification is impossible AND naming both verifier-identities (per amendment b); (iii) accept residual provenance gap explicitly. The discipline is OPERATOR-MANUAL — NO T3/T4/T6 hook currently parses `non-automatable: true` as BLOCKING. Promotion to mechanical BLOCKING is FORWARD-REF for a separate enforcement-hook ship: `tools/non_automatable_blocking_audit.py` parsing committed rule edits + emitting BLOCKING status in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape + regression tests at `tests/test_non_automatable_blocking_audit.py`. Do NOT claim BLOCKING semantic in commit bodies until that ship lands. Forward-only convention codified Wave 18 PSW arc — Bundle B Round-2 commit `d6acfb1`, Bundle B Round-3 commit `217430f`, karpathy 5-author commit `0fe6282`, citation-discipline Round-2 DEMOTE commit `1b87a6e`, citation-discipline Round-3 hard-gate commit `d07ac71`, this Round-4 prose-downgrade commit closes the architectural concern per `reference_wave18_psw_arc_close_4ship_2026_05_04.md` + Outcome A monotone-decline path per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` (R1 conf=0.86 → R2 conf=0.88 ESCALATING → R3 conf=0.82 DECLINING → R4 conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS DECLINING-STABLE; severity stable medium; severity-gate satisfied via concrete-verification mechanism per amendments a+b + prose-downgrade per amendment c; arc converging within Round 5 ceiling).

35: 7. **Anthropic-bundled-skill cites: cite the current slash command, not local folder names** — *(specialization of rule #4 for bundled-skill identifier divergence)* — Bundled skill identifiers can diverge across local folders, CCBP tables, changelogs, and official docs. Example: CCBP `changelog/best-practice/concepts/changelog.md:410 @ 76599cb` records a stale-name correction from `/fewer-permission-prompts` to `/less-permission-prompts`, while CCBP `best-practice/claude-skills.md:48 @ 76599cb` still lists the folder-style `fewer-permission-prompts`, AND the current official Commands page (`code.claude.com/docs/en/commands` line 90 [VERIFIED 2026-05-01 via codex T1 web-fetch]) shows `/fewer-permission-prompts` — so the three authoritative surfaces actively disagree. **Discipline**: when sss documents an Anthropic-bundled skill invocation, cite the current command from `code.claude.com/docs/en/commands` FIRST as the tie-breaker; use `code.claude.com/docs/en/skills` only for general skill mechanics; use CCBP tables/changelogs as secondary drift evidence when they conflict. Never treat `.claude/skills/<folder>` or plugin cache paths as proof of the user-visible slash command. Apply to: `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/agents/*.md`, `.claude/commands/**`, `.claude/skills/**/SKILL.md`, `.claude/rules/**`, `docs/sota-grand-catalog/**`, active adoption/citation docs, and commit bodies citing skill invocations. n=1 per `codification-threshold.md`: observed live conflict across official Commands docs / official Skills docs / CCBP changelog / CCBP skills table / sss docs costs ~15 min to re-derive per stale cite, this rule is <200 LOC; re-evaluate at the next Anthropic bundled-skill rename or official/CCBP conflict.

37: 8. **Composed-claims discipline + mechanical source-class reduction lattice** — *(Wave 17 fire-31 v1 codification per codex T1 fire-28 conf=0.87; UPGRADED at fire-36 per codex T1 fire-33 NEEDS-REVISION on T2 b8fa7dd1 finding "weakest-source rule not computable" conf=0.72)* — When a single normative claim cites MULTIPLE source classes, the cite MUST declare:
38:    - `constituents=[<class1@cite1>, <class2@cite2>, ...]` — explicit list of each constituent primitive at file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL
39:    - `effective_tier=<class>` — the EFFECTIVE class of the composed claim, computed by the reduction lattice below
40: 
41:    **Source-class precedence/order (lower wins; weakest dominates)**:
42:    1. `UNKNOWN` (no cite at all) — always disqualifying; reject claim until cited
43:    2. `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` (any local glue / sss-novel composition over TIER-1/TIER-2 substrates)
44:    3. `TIER-3-LOCAL-OPERATOR-DERIVED` (sss dogfood, codex verdicts, incidents, operator decisions)
45:    4. `TIER-3-LOCAL-CONFIG` (sss live config or script wiring)
46:    5. `TIER-2` (user-curated/sibling docs, NOT upstream authority)
47:    6. `TIER-1-COMPOSED` (ALL constituents TIER-1 with disclosed local glue) — still TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION as effective tier
48:    7. `TIER-1-DIRECT` (upstream verbatim file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL with anchor)
49: 
50:    **Reduction rule (mechanical)**: `effective_tier = MIN_PRECEDENCE(constituents)`. Concretely: if ANY constituent contains "TIER-3-LOCAL-*" OR the composition glue itself is local-codified (e.g., sss-novel composition pattern), the effective tier is `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` regardless of how many TIER-1 primitives are cited. EXCEPTION: if the composed claim is ONLY about an upstream primitive's EXISTENCE (e.g., "asyncRewake field exists" cited verbatim from Anthropic docs), it remains `TIER-1-DIRECT` because there's no local composition glue.
51: 
52:    **Anti-promotion rule**: composition CANNOT promote a TIER-2/TIER-3 component into TIER-1. The cite class of the COMPOSED claim is determined by the MIN_PRECEDENCE constituent OR by the composition-glue class, whichever is weaker.
53: 
54:    **Exemplar (computable)**: `.claude/rules/cross-model-consensus.md §Normative Authority Map` row "T1-T7 touchpoint lifecycle":
55:    - `constituents=[TIER-1-DIRECT @ Anthropic-CC-hooks-docs-L1021-1023, TIER-2 @ CCBP-cross-model-workflow.md:1-48, TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION @ sss-novel T0+T7+profile-selection]`
56:    - `effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` (MIN_PRECEDENCE = 2 due to sss-novel glue)
57:    - Despite Anthropic CC + CCBP being upstream-authority for primitives, the composition itself is sss-local and EFFECTIVE class is TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION
58: 
59:    Apply to: any rule body section in `.claude/rules/**` that mixes upstream + sister-rule + local-operator-codification claims. Sister: `cross-model-consensus.md §Normative Authority Map` (POINTS TO this rule, does NOT carry softer duplicate).
60:

63: - `@latest` on npm may hit Windows cleanup bug → install succeeds but arborist crashes on EPERM (parent `project_gitnexus_full_audit_2026_04_18.md` — PARENT-ATTRIBUTION); prefer `npm install -g` for CLI-bundled MCP servers

docs\cosign-verify-attestation-discipline.md:104:**Azure-specific clarification (Pattern A fix-forward edit #3 per codex T1 #3 prescription)**: Azure KMS/service identities DO NOT use the Google-style `<account>@<project>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` SAN format. Azure identities typically use Azure AD object IDs OR Service Principal client IDs as SAN; issuer is `https://login.microsoftonline.com/<tenant>`. Always discover Azure SAN format from the actual cert; do NOT extrapolate from Google format.

docs\cosign-verify-attestation-discipline.md:242:**Pre-release / Release-candidate handling note (Pattern A fix-forward edit #9 per codex T1 #9 prescription)**: pre-release artifacts (alpha / beta / rc / nightly / -SNAPSHOT) are NOT categorically excluded from this discipline. docs\cosign-verify-attestation-discipline.md:252:- **Accept a valid Sigstore signature WITHOUT checking artifact digest + cert identity against expected upstream** (Pattern A fix-forward edit #7 per codex T1 #7 prescription) — refuted: Sigstore signatures only attest "this artifact was signed by this identity at this time"; they do NOT validate the artifact is FROM your expected upstream/repo/workflow. Verification logic MUST cross-check: (a) artifact SHA-256 digest matches expected hash, (b) certificate subject matches `^https://github\.com/<expected-owner>/<expected-repo>/` regex, (c) workflow path matches `.github/workflows/<expected-workflow>.yml@` when known. Missing any cross-check = effectively unsigned.
docs\cosign-verify-attestation-discipline.md:253:- **Use broad `--certificate-identity-regexp` matching forks / unrelated workflows / user-controlled refs when tighter identity is knowable** (Pattern A fix-forward edit #8 per codex T1 #8 prescription) — refuted: open regexp like `^https://github\.com/<owner>/<repo>/` matches ALL workflows in repo including PR-checks workflows, dependabot workflows, fork-rebased workflows (if attacker submits a PR with malicious release workflow). When the expected workflow path IS known (e.g., `.github/workflows/release.yml`), MANDATE tightening: `^https://github\.com/<owner>/<repo>/\.github/workflows/release\.yml@refs/tags/v\d+\.\d+\.\d+$`. Forbid org-only OR unanchored regexps unless explicit provenance documentation justifies (e.g., multi-workflow release strategy intentionally varies workflow path).

docs\4class-memory-taxonomy-discipline.md:71:## Boundary precedence (Pattern A fix-forward edit #2 per codex T1 prescription)
docs\4class-memory-taxonomy-discipline.md:93:  │           → Gate 4 Trace Replay GATED: cycle-322 ≥3-dogfood evidence
docs\4class-memory-taxonomy-discipline.md:104:- **Auto-promote PROCEDURAL-SKILL-OR-RULE without Gate 4 evidence** — refuted by Fire 29c GPT-5.5 Axis-4 + cycle-322 jurisdiction (n≥3 self-observed OR user-trigger explicit). Skipping Gate 4 for procedural promotion produces premature codification (rule gets shipped on n=1 self-observed evidence; later refuted).
docs\4class-memory-taxonomy-discipline.md:106:- **Auto-promote EPISODIC-TRACE to PROCEDURAL** — refuted by Fire 29c "promote knowledge only when evidence SURVIVES REPLAY". One episode is n=1; promoting to procedural rule requires n=3 dogfood per cycle-322. Episode is evidence, not procedure.
docs\4class-memory-taxonomy-discipline.md:109:- **Promote OPERATIONAL-RUNBOOK directly to PROCEDURAL without intermediate evidence-synthesis** — refuted by cycle-322 + evidence-synthesis discipline below. See §Evidence-synthesis ladder (Pattern A fix-forward edit #3 + #5): promotion does NOT mutate the source record; it creates a NEW higher-order record (Class 1 or Class 3) with backlinks to supporting Class 4 / Class 2 records. The ladder is therefore an EVIDENCE-SYNTHESIS ladder, NOT a reclassification ladder.
docs\4class-memory-taxonomy-discipline.md:110:- **Reclassify a memory record by changing its class label** (Pattern A fix-forward edit #5 per codex T1 prescription) — refuted by storage-class-immutability. A SEMANTIC-FACT record once written stays Class 1 even after additional evidence accumulates; what changes is that a SEPARATE new record at a different class is created. .claude\rules\multi-source-discovery-breadth-discipline.md:17:**Distinct source definition** (per Pattern A codex T1 Fire 29a prescriptions #1+#2+#6): A "distinct source" is a distinct **backend / data corpus / provider**, NOT a tool endpoint. Multiple MCP endpoints OR CLI commands under the SAME provider count as ONE source family. Example: `mcp__github__search_repositories` + `mcp__github__search_code` + `mcp__github__list_commits` + `gh CLI` all count as ONE source (GitHub).

.claude\rules\multi-source-discovery-breadth-discipline.md:88:- **Counting multiple endpoints from one provider as multiple distinct sources** (per Pattern A codex T1 Fire 29a prescription #6) — refuted by §Discovery cohort "Distinct source definition" + §Counting rules: a single provider (GitHub / DeepWiki / Exa / Context7 / repomix) collapses ALL its sub-capabilities into ONE source-family count. Example anti-pattern: querying `mcp__github__search_repositories` + `mcp__github__search_code` + `mcp__github__list_commits` + `gh CLI repo view` and claiming "4 sources" — actual count = 1 source (GitHub family). Must query ≥4 DIFFERENT provider families.
.claude\rules\multi-source-discovery-breadth-discipline.md:89:- **Counting INSTALLED-NOT-WIRED sources as available without verifying alternate path** (per Pattern A codex T1 Fire 29a prescription #4+#5) — refuted by §Counting rules: NOT-WIRED sources require documented working alternate path (WebFetch / HTTP-direct / ctx_fetch_and_index) AND relevant-results return. Example anti-pattern: Perplexity@0.8.4 npm-global is INSTALLED but NOT in current .mcp.json; cannot count as "MCP source" until either wired OR queried via WebFetch fallback with relevant results.

2: name: Codex T1 single-fix-forward + timeout-trace-mining pattern
3: description: Two empirically-validated patterns for the codex T1 pre-edit consult lifecycle. Pattern A (n=5+) — NEEDS-REVISION verdict at conf 0.88-0.93 → apply ALL prescribed_edits in single atomic commit. Pattern B (n=3) — T1 timeout-without-JSON-verdict → trace-mine for embedded evidence + ship as-designed + T3 verify. Both patterns honor cross-model-consensus.md HARD GATE and cycle-300 ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE.
4: paths: [".claude/state/codex_consult_*.txt", ".claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt", ".claude/rules/cross-model-consensus.md", ".claude/rules/closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md", ".claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md"]

7: # Codex T1 single-fix-forward + timeout-trace-mining pattern
8: 
9: # TIER-3 local codification provenance only: promoted from
10: # feedback_iter15_21_codex_t1_arc_pattern.md (n=5 evidence) +
11: # feedback_codex_t1_timeout_extract_partial_evidence.md (n=3 evidence) per
12: # cycle-322 jurisdiction (n≥3 promotes feedback→rule). Feedback memories and
13: # codex_consult outputs are evidence trails, not SOTA design authority.
14: 
15: The codex T1 pre-edit consult discipline (per `cross-model-consensus.md`) produces
16: TWO distinct lifecycle outcomes that both honor the HARD GATE. This rule
17: codifies both with explicit branching disposition so neither becomes a
18: silent-fallback or false-failure mode.
19: 
20: ## Pattern signature
21: 
22: ```
23:                   ┌──────────────────┐
24:                   │ Build T1 consult │
25:                   │ (axes + options) │
26:                   └────────┬─────────┘
27:                            │
28:                            ▼
29:                   ┌──────────────────┐
30:                   │ Fire codex exec  │
31:                   │  --ephemeral     │
32:                   │  -p deep-review  │
33:                   │  background poll │
34:                   └────────┬─────────┘
35:                            │
36:               ┌────────────┴────────────┐
37:               ▼                         ▼
38:    ┌──────────────────┐      ┌──────────────────┐
39:    │ Pattern A        │      │ Pattern B        │
40:    │ JSON verdict     │      │ Timeout 240s,    │
41:    │ APPROVE or       │      │ no JSON, trace   │
42:    │ NEEDS-REVISION   │      │ contains evidence│
43:    │ + prescriptions  │      │ (or sandbox      │
44:    │                  │      │  blocked reads)  │
45:    └────────┬─────────┘      └────────┬─────────┘
46:             │                         │
47:             ▼                         ▼
48:    Apply ALL prescriptions    HONEST-NON-FINDING:
49:    in SINGLE atomic commit    mine trace, ship per
50:    (no iter-N→iter-N.1)       prior-fire research,
51:             │                  T3 post-commit verify
52:             ▼                         │
53:    T3 postcommit auto-fire ◄──────────┘
54:    verifies APPROVE
55: ```
56: 
57: ## Pattern A — NEEDS-REVISION → single fix-forward (n=5 promotion-eligible)
58: 
59: **Trigger**: codex T1 returns JSON verdict `NEEDS-REVISION` with confidence 0.88-0.93
60: and ≤10 `prescribed_edits`.
61: 
62: **Disposition**: apply ALL prescriptions in a SINGLE atomic commit per
63: `cycle-300` ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE. Do NOT iterate iter-N→iter-N.1→iter-N.2
64: — that's the `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` cycle-539 layered-correction
65: anti-pattern. Verify via `git diff` per `synthesis-layer-verify.md` Mia rule
66: before commit.
67: 
68: **Why this works**: Codex deep-review profile is calibrated to find structural
69: defects in concrete proposals. Verdicts at conf 0.88-0.93 with 5-10
70: prescribed_edits = the "sweet spot" — high-confidence corrections, manageable
71: single-pass apply. The fix-forward applies cleanly and T3 postcommit (when
72: wired per iter-23 PostToolUse) typically returns APPROVE on the same commit.
73: 
74: **Empirical evidence trail (n=5)**:
75: 
76: | iter | T1 conf | prescriptions | result | commit |
77: |---|---|---|---|---|
78: | iter-18 (P4.b prepush port) | 0.91 | 10 | applied → no further fixes | 2a43eea |
79: | iter-19 (P5.a t1_gate port + wire) | 0.91 | 7 | applied → smoke probe 5/5 pass | 927532a |
80: | iter-20 (P6.a recursive P2 rescue) | 0.93 | 6 | applied → n-counter incremented | 2c74585 |
81: | iter-21 (P7.a sss activation) | 0.91 | 7 (selected_option=B) | applied → no further fixes | 9a09008 |
82: | iter-22 (P9 .mcp.json bootstrap) | 0.91 | 8 (selected_option=C) | applied → no further fixes | 1209786 |
83: 
84: Promotion-trigger satisfied at iter-22 (5th instance at conf 0.88-0.93 + ≤10
85: prescriptions). This rule IS the codification.
86: 
87: ## Pattern B — T1 timeout-without-JSON-verdict → trace-mine + ship (n=3 evidence)
88: 
89: **Trigger**: codex T1 fired with valid 2-3-axis prompt but the 240s
90: `codex exec --ephemeral -p deep-review --color never` budget exhausts BEFORE
91: the agent emits a `{"verdict": ...}` JSON payload. Two known sub-causes:
92: 
93: 1. **Active deep-research mode**: codex spends budget on web search +
94:    file reads investigating axes. Trace contains useful embedded evidence
95:    (URLs fetched, file:line citations) but no structured verdict.
96: 2. **Sandbox-policy-blocked reads** (NEW-CLASS observed iter-26): codex T1
97:    runs with `sandbox=read-only` profile that rejects pwsh.exe-based file
98:    reads. Codex retries 2-3 pwsh patterns, all rejected, budget exhausts
99:    without substantive review.
100: 
101: **Disposition**: per TIER-3 local pattern provenance in `feedback_codex_t1_timeout_extract_partial_evidence.md`
102: HONEST-NON-FINDING reporting category (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §3
103: 3-category framing): record T1 fired without verdict; mine trace for any
104: embedded line-cited evidence; proceed with ship per prior-fire research +
105: standing-directive defaults; T2 (`codex exec review --uncommitted`) and/or
106: T3 (postcommit auto via iter-23 wire) provide the cross-model verification
107: shifted to commit time.
108: 
109: **DO NOT loop iter-N→iter-N.1→iter-N.2 chasing a verdict** — the timeout
110: state is a signal about the prompt or sandbox, not the design. Per
111: cycle-907 standing directive (auto-proceed within authorized scope):
112: treat as fired-without-verdict, ship, verify on commit.
113: 
114: **Empirical evidence trail (n=3)**:
115: 
116: | iter | T1 prompt axes | timeout cause | trace evidence used | commit |
117: |---|---|---|---|---|
118: | iter-23 (T3+T4 wire) | 5 axes (broad) | active deep-research | upstream settings.json read, dependency import graph | db94b27 |
119: | iter-25 (env-var script) | 3 axes (security/scope/codex-auth) | active web-search of codex auth docs | none — codex was researching the right axis but never structured | eb6131f |
120: | iter-26 (T6 stop-gate port) | 2 axes (TIGHT) | sandbox blocked pwsh.exe reads (3 rejected) | none — codex never reached file content | af54fef |
121: 
122: n=3 evidence triggers cycle-322 promotion to rule layer (this rule). Pattern B
123: is now binding discipline alongside Pattern A.
124: 
125: ## When this discipline applies
126: 
127: Both patterns apply to ANY /loop iter or substantive edit on a design surface
128: where codex T1 is fired. Gate path scope is default-in-scope and skips only
129: `_UNIVERSAL_EXCLUSIONS` per `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:23-28,104-108,235`.
130: Common in-scope examples (NOT exhaustive):
131: 
132: - `.claude/hooks/scripts/*.py`
133: - `.claude/rules/*.md`
134: - `.claude/agents/*.md`
135: - `.claude/commands/*.md`
136: - `.claude/skills/**/SKILL.md`
137: - `.claude/schemas/*.json`
138: - `.claude/settings.json`
139: - `.mcp.json`
140: - `CLAUDE*.md`
141: - `PINS.json`
142: - `tools/sss.ps1`, `bin/sss.cmd`
143: 
144: Plus any single logical unit per cycle-300 ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE where
145: codex preflight (`_codex_preflight.py` per upstream) returns ok.
146: 
147: ## How to apply
148: 
149: 1. **Build T1 consult** at `.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>.txt`:
150:    - User directive verbatim
151:    - Context (prior fire findings + current state)
152:    - Proposed edit (or 2-3 options if uncertain — codex picks)
153:    - 2-3 explicit AXIS-N audit dimensions (TIGHT — see §Update triggers
154:      below for n=3 timeout learning)
155:    - Output schema (JSON-strict per `cross-model-consensus.md`)
156:    - Hint codex to use direct file reads (`rg`, `grep`) NOT
157:      `pwsh -Command` patterns (sandbox-blocked per Pattern B sub-cause 2)
158: 
159: 2. **Fire codex T1 in background**:
160:    ```bash
161:    timeout 240 codex exec --ephemeral -p deep-review --color never \
162:      < .claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>.txt \
163:      > .claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt 2>&1 &
164:    ```
165: 
166:    **Pair-basename gate-compatibility note**: the prompt basename MUST equal the output basename before `_OUT`. `codex_t1_consult_gate.py:_consult_pairs()` strips `_OUT` from output filenames and looks for a prompt at exactly that stem. **`codex_consult_<topic>_PROMPT.txt` does NOT pair with `codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt`** — use canonical `<topic>.txt + <topic>_OUT.txt`. Prompts in `tmp/` are also invisible to the gate (it only globs `.claude/state/`). FM-08 root cause: my prompts in `tmp/iter*_t1_consult_*.txt` (gitignored) + verdicts in `.claude/state/<topic>_OUT.txt` produced n=14+ WARN false-positives across iters 63-66 because the prompt half of the pair was missing from `.claude/state/`.
167: 
168: 3. **While T1 runs, do PREP**: read source files, smoke probes, design.
169: 
170: 4. **Read T1 verdict** (search for `"verdict"` JSON key in OUT file tail):
171: 
172:    - **Pattern A path**: JSON verdict found at `verdict ∈ {APPROVE, NEEDS-REVISION, REJECT}`:
173:      - APPROVE conf ≥0.90 → ship as-proposed
174:      - NEEDS-REVISION conf ≥0.85 → apply ALL prescribed_edits in SINGLE commit
175:      - REJECT or conf <0.85 → STOP, escalate to user
176: 
177:    - **Pattern B path**: NO JSON verdict in tail (timeout, sandbox-blocked, etc.):
178:      - Mine trace for line-cited evidence (rg outputs, web URLs, file content)
179:      - Proceed with ship per prior-fire research + standing-directive defaults
180:      - Document trace evidence in commit body (which axes WERE substantively reviewed)
